# Findings ledger

The canonical key-findings ledger for this repository. Every material claim
has exactly one canonical home in a subsystem report; other documents
summarize it in one sentence and link there. This ledger records the
**key** findings per subsystem — it is not an exhaustive per-function index
(for that see `data/semantic_coverage_ledger.csv`).

## Evidence model

Evidence has two dimensions: **source** (where the observation came from) and
**confidence** (how strongly it is established). Keep them separate — a
Corolla field observation is a real, direct observation (`observed`) that is
nonetheless not reproduced from firmware bytes.

### Evidence source

| Source | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **firmware-static** | Recovered from the committed firmware bytes |
| **dynamic-probe** | Observed on a live vehicle / bus |
| **generated-artifact** | Produced by a `data/` generator |
| **external-source** | From an outside document, report, or third party |

### Confidence grade

| Grade | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **verified** | Directly asserted by a deterministic test in `tests/` |
| **observed** | Directly observed (e.g. a field probe) but not reproduced by a repository test |
| **recovered** | Control/data flow substantially reconstructed; not claimed as behaviorally understood |
| **bounded** | Interpretation constrained, exact semantics unknown |
| **hypothesis** | Plausible, explicitly unverified |
| **disproved** | Retained only to prevent regression; see [CORRECTIONS.md](CORRECTIONS.md) |

### Mapping the variant CSV vocabulary

`data/tss3_eps_variant_matrix.csv` (and `tests/verify_tss3_variant_matrix.py`)
use a coarser per-row grade. The mapping onto this model is:

| CSV grade | Source | Confidence |
|---|---|---|
| `definitive` | firmware-static | verified |
| `inference` | dynamic-probe / external-source | observed → hypothesis (per field) |
| `none` | — | hypothesis (unobserved) |

The CSV grade is a row-level summary; individual fields within an `inference`
row carry their own confidence on the variant page (e.g. a directly observed
software ID is `observed`, an inferred MCU is `hypothesis`).

| ARCH-016 | Pinned commaai/opendbc Toyota prior art defines vehicle support as a generation-specific **control contract**, not a CAN-ID list: platform flags select source-ECU architecture; `CarState` supplies steering/cruise/readiness/fault feedback; `CarController` combines command cadence with driver/EPS limits; Panda substitution suppresses the stock producer; and SecOC wraps only the protected semantic streams. The concrete older contract is `0x2E4` `STEERING_LKA`, TSS2 `0x191` `STEERING_LTA` plus secure `0x131` `STEERING_LTA_2`, `0x343` `ACC_CONTROL` plus secure `0x183` `ACC_CONTROL_2`, feedback `0x025/0x260/0x262`, and UI `0x412`. Target-native evidence proves the **roles**, not those wire identities, are the transferable roadmap: exact Corolla H lacks active `0x2E4/0x131` EPS profiles and its route carries a 64-byte CAN-FD `0x183`, so old IDs/shapes must not be projected onto TSS3. Production TSS3 support therefore requires recovering command + feedback + producer/route + stock suppression + limits/faults + UI + authentication per generation; `FRC_P5` firmware is the current highest-value producer-side acquisition. | comma Toyota prior art + Sienna/Corolla target boundary | pinned external implementation + verified local counterexamples; TSS3 wire contract open | `verify_toyota_porting_contract.py`; optional `verify_external_corroboration.py` | [../architecture/toyota-openpilot-porting-contract.md](../architecture/toyota-openpilot-porting-contract.md) |

The delay is **10 seconds**: `0x1D24` reads `TAUJ1CNT0` (`FFE51010`), `0x1C60` programs `TAUJ1TPS=FFF2` (`PRS0=2`, PCLK/4) and `TAUJ1CMOR0=0156` (CK0 count clock), and the P1M-E peripheral/P-Bus domain is 80 MHz, yielding a 20-MHz counter. `0x55AA` separately arms the same delay during boot diagnostic initialization, but normal application→PROGRAMMING replay uses the retained `{kind=0,id=7A1,session=2}` record at `31914` and `6504→5148→562A` explicitly clears `FEBF2B56` before synthetic boot `10 02`; Calvin's ~1-s successful unlocks therefore corroborate this clear path rather than contradict the post-failure lockout. All state is LocalRAM-only. ## Memory safety

| ID | Claim | Scope | Grade | Checked by | Canonical report |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| MEM-SAFE-001 | `payload_decrypt_transfer_task @ 0x6BDE` processes `floor(byte_count/16)` AES-CBC blocks; lengths 1–15 execute zero blocks yet subtract the full length and mark complete. The raw request bytes are then copied to the download destination. TransferData (`0x4B7C`) accepts final chunks of 1–0x400 without 16-byte alignment, so a tester who has completed one prior `0x10F0` authentication can write arbitrary raw bytes into `0xFEBF0000..0xFEBF0FFF` (including the callback pointer at `0xFEBF0FD0`) and invoke `0xFF00` without another CMAC. This is a missing-alignment defect, not an OOB write — `boot_memory_range_check_access @ 0x32D2` rejects zero length, address wrap, and out-of-window ranges. The operational helper uses one RequestDownload/short TransferData/exit triplet per ≤15-byte chunk and requires an explicit same-session prior-auth assertion | Sienna | verified | `verify_memory_safety.py`, `verify_memory_safety_mutations.py`, `verify_exploit_followups.py`, `AssertMemorySafetyPaths.java` | [../security/memory-safety-audit.md](../security/memory-safety-audit.md) |
| MEM-SAFE-002 | `payload_cmac_verify_setup @ 0x7122` computes `end = start + length - 16`; `payload_cmac_verify_step @ 0x7170` advances by 16 and only matches final at `end`. For `length % 16 != 0` the endpoint is never reached, causing an OOB read past the requested range. For `length < 16` the endpoint underflows below `start`. Of lengths `1..0x1000`, exactly 256 terminate and 3,840 walk OOB. The exact step-function callee boundary contains only CMAC primitive `0x7E0C`; no direct exfiltration consumer exists in that bounded graph. Reachable after a CRC-only success (constructible via MEM-SAFE-001), but closed as availability-only absent a concrete side channel/consumer | Sienna | verified | `verify_memory_safety.py`, `verify_memory_safety_mutations.py`, `verify_exploit_followups.py`, `AssertMemorySafetyPaths.java` | [../security/memory-safety-audit.md](../security/memory-safety-audit.md) |
| MEM-SAFE-003 | RID `0x10F3` arms a compare-mode RequestDownload (operation bit 5); `memory_compare_task @ 0x6C8E` byte-compares tester data against CodeFlash. Equality → positive response, mismatch → NRC `0x10`. Sub-16-byte chunks are not decrypted (MEM-SAFE-001), enabling single-byte guesses. Accessible ranges are exactly `0x10000..0x17DFF` and `0x18000..0xFFDFF`; bootloader secrets at `0xBFD8/0xBFE8` are unreachable. The bounded host implementation re-arms every guess, fails closed on every non-`0x10` error, and deterministically recovers canonical-image bytes; worst case remains 256 requests per byte | Sienna | verified | `verify_memory_safety.py`, `verify_memory_safety_mutations.py`, `verify_exploit_followups.py`, `AssertMemorySafetyPaths.java` | [../security/memory-safety-audit.md](../security/memory-safety-audit.md) |
| MEM-SAFE-004 | ICU-S command-8 failure path (`0x86EE8`) zero-fills the caller's output buffer using the unbounded original length (`0x89044`). The configured worker at `0x6823C` fixes output capacity at 48; the driver-dispatch call at `0x6828A` is unique, and exact caller censuses expose no remotely controllable pointer/length in this firmware graph. Random command-8 stimulation cannot change those CPU-side arguments. Reopen only for a variant caller above 48 or independent corruption reaching the prepare API | Sienna | verified (latent) | `verify_memory_safety.py`, `verify_memory_safety_mutations.py`, `verify_exploit_followups.py`, `AssertMemorySafetyPaths.java` | [../security/memory-safety-audit.md](../security/memory-safety-audit.md) |
| MEM-SAFE-005 | No corruption was found inside the enumerated ISO-TP, CAN receive, application-copy, SecOC receive, and bootloader range-check boundary. Deterministic checks lock the sole application-copy caller gate, canonical DLC table, six SecOC record lengths, pre-subtraction trailer gate, and zero/wrap range rejection. This is not an unqualified whole-image absence claim | Sienna | bounded | `verify_memory_safety.py`, `verify_memory_safety_mutations.py`, `AssertMemorySafetyPaths.java` | [../security/memory-safety-audit.md](../security/memory-safety-audit.md) |
| MEM-SAFE-006 | The application RDBI render machinery (`9429E -> 929B0/92810 -> 4CB8A` callbacks) checks response capacity **after** the producer runs, a check-after-copy shape that invites an overflow reading. The shape yields **no over-writing producer** in this calibration, now closed for the full configured surface: `944C6` accepts exactly one DID per RDBI request (payload even, >=2, `length>>1 <= 1`); the preflight driver `94426 -> 9404A` accumulates `declared_len + 2` into `FEBE5D4C` from the **same fixed DID-table record word** (`0x2941C + 16*idx + 2`) that the render path's length provider `4C81A` returns; and the emitted count is configuration-owned, never producer-returned — `0x8A374` writes the configured length word into the count slot *before* dispatching the producer through `0x4CB8A` as `callback(dest, declared_len)`, and the render loop advances by that slot. The deterministic emitted-write audit classifies all **196 unique configured producers** from firmware bytes plus the hash-pinned decompiler corpus: 134 fixed-offset store-only bodies, 46 four-byte success stubs (write nothing), 11 engine wrappers whose every write is guarded by the forwarded declared_len, 3 fixed-extent checkpoint/software-ID loops, 1 declared-length-bounded loop (F18C), and 1 single-byte register delegate (F186). **No producer's write extent exceeds its declared length** (150 exact-fit, 46 zero-write; the only DIDs written short are exactly the verified 48-DID stale census). | MEM-SAFE-007 | Application SID `0x23` RMBA is a large SecurityAccess-free **read disclosure**, but its configured software path is closed against the targeted memory-safety escapes. Only ALFID `0x15` exists: with memory-ID mode enabled, request data is `15 ‖ memid[1] ‖ address_be32[4] ‖ size[1]`, so size is `1..255`. Range selection first requires memory ID `1/2` and inclusive address membership (`FEBE0000..FEBFFFFF` / `FF200000..FF207FFF`); end fit uses unsigned `high-address >= size-1`, avoiding addition wrap, and the copy-time validators independently reject overlap with all five RAM/two DataFlash exclusions. The apparent async/TOCTOU branch in `8C456` is unreachable for configured IDs because it is selected by `memid>>4`; IDs `1/2` force the synchronous bounded `4EB1C` reader, and the parsed address/size/class cells have no unrelated whole-image writers. Both memory classes have zero write-range entries. Verified negative for overflow/wrap/range-crossing/TOCTOU escape from the recovered RMBA read allow-set; not a whole-Dcm safety claim and does not reduce SEC-APP-006 disclosure impact | Sienna | verified | `verify_application_rmba_memory_safety.py`; `verify_application_read_memory_by_address.py` | [../diagnostics/application.md](../diagnostics/application.md) |

| SECOC-033 | End-to-end Panda tracing recovers the missing dimension in yc's Toyota-B swap experiment. `UdsClient.bus` selects only a logical Panda queue: logical bus 1 maps to MCU FDCAN2, while harness orientation swaps only logical 0/2. Independently, `SAFETY_ELM327` param 0 selects `CAN_MODE_OBD_CAN2` and param 1/nonzero selects `CAN_MODE_NORMAL`; on comma 4/Cuatro, inherited `tres_set_can_mode` connects the same FDCAN2 to one of two GPIO/transceiver paths (normal orientation: param1 -> PB5/PB6+transceiver2, param0 -> PB12/PB13+transceiver4; flipped orientation reverses the implementing pins/transceiver while preserving normal-vs-OBD semantics). ELM policy itself ignores the parameter and permits `0x7A1` under either mode. `Panda.set_obd()` is only a temporary board-mode USB control and does not update remembered `current_safety_param`; harness-status changes reapply `set_safety_mode(current_safety_mode,current_safety_param)`, so the robust normal-route configuration is `set_safety_mode(3,1)`, not implicit-param0 followed by `set_obd(False)`. Normal openpilot `PandaRunner` uses exactly this param1 baseline and toggles OBD separately. The Bk2ol probe/dumper instead hardcodes `BUS=0` plus implicit ELM param0, its collector ignores bus1, and changing only `BUS=1` therefore keeps FDCAN2 on the OBD physical path. For the reported stock Toyota-B CAN0/CAN1 repin case, **`ELM327 param 1 + logical bus 1` is the recovered high-confidence direct-diagnostic-route candidate**; CORR-072/VAR-006 now bound the separate official harness dimension: physical repinning moves the vehicle network onto the CAN0/CAN2 intercept-relay pair, while param1+bus1 leaves it on unsplit CAN1 and is therefore not a full relay-topology equivalent. Live programming-transition confirmation still separates the remaining external OBD/gateway/timing/ACK causes. ELM also leaves the physical intercept relay pass-through while `nooutput_init` disables software forwarding, correcting the earlier overstatement that active 0<->2 forwarding itself explained the dump. Independently, `8965B4512000` firmware still disproves an EPS controller switch: application and boot `0x7A1/0x7A9` remain RSCFD channel 1. Techstream P4-family databases label base U023A as lost front-camera/image-processing communication. | SECOC-035 | The blurbdust/yc 8-byte egg must remain a location signature, not a semantic SecOC signature. A raw analyzer now reports only image hash, every egg occurrence, context, and exact `01 52 7F 00` immediate-success replacement; caller attribution is intentionally delegated to an instruction-aware Ghidra script. This design was itself validated on `8965B4512000`: naive raw short-JARL scanning produced 11 apparent candidate callers, whereas Ghidra proves exactly two direct call references to `0x3485A`; corrected service ownership places both in SID `0xBA` token/operation logic, with zero direct ICU-S references. Official comma hardware maps harness-box CAN0=CAR, CAN1=RADAR, CAN2=CAMERA, CAN3=COMMA POWER and places the solid-state intercept relay specifically between CAN0/CAN2; the Toyota-B adapter presents CAN2+CAN1 camera-side and CAN0+CAN1 car-side. Separately, the old bus-only diagnostic attempt retained ELM param 0, so logical bus 1/FDCAN2 remained muxed to the OBD path; Panda statically provides a direct stock-wire diagnostic route with `ELM param 1 + bus 1`, but that does not recreate 0/2 relay interception. Exact `8965H1202000` firmware eliminates an EPS app→boot bus migration: application and boot are both RSCFD channel 1 with boot `0x7A1/0x777 -> 0x7A9`, the application RSCFD maps/config transfer byte-identically from Sienna, boot init is byte-identical and core boot CAN/CanIf code differs only at three relocation bytes. Its PROGRAMMING session also transfers the asynchronous reset handoff and `0x0180/0x0A00` policy thresholds, so timeout alone cannot prove rejection. An independent raw-directory walk plus immutable record fixture and pinned consumer extents prove the UTF-16 code, packed `(base_dtc << 8) \| failure_type` at `+0x2C`, base-description index at `+0x30`, and failure-text index at `+0x34`. | TMS-041 | The category-498 `FRC_P5` steering Active-Test branch is closed as **fixed routine control, not a parameterized lateral writer**. NA/EU/JP `FRC_P5.ddb` contain no type-68 `CDbActTestP5Table`; steering-relevant entries are type-71 `CDbRoutineActTestP5Table` records: `LDA Steering Vibration` RID `0x1508`, `LTA Steering Vibration` `0x1588`, `LCA Steering Vibration` `0x15C8`, and `AES Automatic Steering in Control Notification` `0x160B`. Machine-code consumers pin type-71 `+0x1C` as routine ID, `+0x28/+0x2A/+0x2C` as variable-table references returned by `GetRoutineCommand`/`GetOutputMaskValue`/`GetOutputMaskButtonData`, `+0x2E` as routine-status key, and `+0x38` as sort key. All four steering-related records have command/mask/button refs zero; the three vibration rows use status key 2, whose type-72 row resolves master variable `0x0054` to byte `02`. `SingleRoutineActTstP5_DT.dll` executes `D5 -> Sleep(200 ms) -> D7 -> Sleep(5000 ms) -> D6`; raw master type-17 records `D5/D6/D7` all resolve send prefix `21 E2` / receive check `61 E2` with masks `0000000800/0000000400/0000000200`. The executor writes RID high/low into request bytes 2/3 and only D5 can append `GetRoutineCommand`; therefore the vibration requests are fixed `21 E2 15 08`, `21 E2 15 88`, `21 E2 15 C8` with no angle/torque/amplitude/setpoint payload. The SingleRoutine/Init/SignalInfo plugin chain has no explicit SecurityAccess/authentication/session-named import, which **does not prove** an outer session/authentication is unnecessary.
